Factor the diagonal block (small, sequential).\n//   2. Solve the panel below the diagonal block (parallel over rows).\n//   3. Update the trailing submatrix A[i][j] -= L[i][k] * L[j][k] (parallel over tiles).\n// The trailing update works on a transposed copy of the panel so that the\n// innermost loop is a contiguous, vectorizable axpy along a row of A.\n\nstatic constexpr size_t CHOL_NB = 64;  // panel width\nstatic constexpr size_t CHOL_TJ = 64;  // tile width (columns) for the trailing update\n\n// Factor the nb x nb diagonal block starting at (jb, jb). Returns false if not PD.\nstatic bool choleskyDiagonalBlock(double* __restrict A, const size_t n, const size_t jb,\n                                  const size_t nb) {\n    for (size_t jj = 0; jj < nb; ++jj) {\n        const size_t j = jb + jj;\n        double* __restrict rowj = A + j * n;\n        double sum = 0.0;\n        for (size_t k = jb; k < j; ++k) {\n            sum += rowj[k] * rowj[k];\n        }\n        const double val = rowj[j] - sum;\n        if (val <= 0.0) {\n            // Matrix is not positive definite\n            printf(\"Error: Matrix is not positive definite at diagonal element %zu\\n\", j);\n            return false;\n        }\n        const double ljj = sqrt(val);\n        rowj[j] = ljj;\n        const double inv = 1.0 / ljj;\n        for (size_t ii = jj + 1; ii < nb; ++ii) {\n            const size_t i = jb + ii;\n            double* __restrict rowi = A + i * n;\n            double s = 0.0;\n            for (size_t k = jb; k < j; ++k) {\n                s += rowi[k] * rowj[k];\n            }\n            rowi[j] = (rowi[j] - s) * inv;\n        }\n    }\n    return true;\n}\n\nbool choleskyDecomposition(std::vector<double>& A, const size_t n) {\n    // A is stored in row-major order\n    double* __restrict a = A.data();\n    const size_t nb = std::min(CHOL_NB, n);\n    bool ok = true;\n\n    // Transposed panel buffer: T[kk][j] = L[j][jb + kk] for j in trailing rows.\n    std::vector<double> Tbuf(nb * n);\n    double* __restrict T = Tbuf.data();\n\n#pragma omp parallel default(shared)\n    {\n        for (size_t jb = 0; jb < n && ok; jb += nb) {\n            const size_t cnb = std::min(nb, n - jb);\n            const size_t rest = jb + cnb;  // first row/col of trailing submatrix\n\n            // 1. Factor diagonal block\n#pragma omp single\n            {\n                if (!choleskyDiagonalBlock(a, n, jb, cnb)) ok = false;\n            }\n            // implicit barrier\n            if (!ok) break;\n\n            // 2. Panel solve: rows i >= rest, columns [jb, rest).\n            //    Forward substitution against the (lower-triangular) diagonal block.\n            //    Also stores the transposed panel into T for the trailing update.\n#pragma omp for schedule(dynamic, 4)\n            for (size_t i = rest; i < n; ++i) {\n                double* __restrict rowi = a + i * n;\n                for (size_t j = jb; j < rest; ++j) {\n                    const double* __restrict rowj = a + j * n;\n                    double s = 0.0;\n                    for (size_t k = jb; k < j; ++k) {\n                        s += rowi[k] * rowj[k];\n                    }\n                    const double lij = (rowi[j] - s) / rowj[j];\n                    rowi[j] = lij;\n                    T[(j - jb) * n + i] = lij;\n                }\n            }\n            // implicit barrier\n\n            // 3. Trailing update: A[i][j] -= sum_k L[i][k] * L[j][k], rest <= j <= i < n.\n            //    Work is distributed over (row, column-tile) pairs for load balance.\n            const size_t ntiles = (n - rest + CHOL_TJ - 1) / CHOL_TJ;\n            const size_t nrows = n - rest;\n#pragma omp for schedule(dynamic, 8) nowait\n            for (size_t w = 0; w < nrows * ntiles; ++w) {\n                const size_t ri = w / ntiles;          // row offset\n                const size_t ct = w % ntiles;          // column tile\n                const size_t i = n - 1 - ri;            // longest rows first\n                const size_t j0 = rest + ct * CHOL_TJ;\n                if (j0 > i) continue;\n                const size_t j1 = std::min(j0 + CHOL_TJ, i + 1);\n                double* __restrict rowi = a + i * n;\n                const double* __restrict lrow = rowi + jb;\n                for (size_t kk = 0; kk < cnb; ++kk) {\n                    const double lik = lrow[kk];\n                    const double* __restrict trow = T + kk * n;\n#pragma omp simd\n                    for (size_t j = j0; j < j1; ++j) {\n                        rowi[j] -= lik * trow[j];\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n#pragma omp barrier\n        }\n\n        // Zero out upper triangular part\n        if (ok) {\n#pragma omp for schedule(static)\n            for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n                for (size_t j = i + 1; j < n; ++j) {\n                    a[i * n + j] = 0.0;\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    return ok;\n}\n\n'''\nsrc = src[:start] + new + src[end:]\n\n# parallelize matrix generation (B*B^T) and validation (L*L^T + reduction)\nsrc = src.replace('''    // Compute A = B * B^T\n    for (size_t i = 0; Factor the diagonal block (small, sequential).\n//   2. Solve the panel below the diagonal block (parallel over rows).\n//   3. j < j1; ++j) {\n                        rowi[j] -= lik * trow[j];\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n#pragma omp barrier\n        }\n\n        // Zero out upper triangular part\n        if (ok) {\n#pragma omp for schedule(static)\n            for (size_t i = 0; {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-fable-5-1","id":"msg_011Cg6L8GLGQj2aJM3N9BYdk","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Done. The Cholesky benchmark in `cholesky/cholesky.cpp` is now a blocked, OpenMP-parallel decomposition, and `cholesky/CMakeLists.txt` links OpenMP unconditionally via `find_package(OpenMP REQUIRED)`. No new files were added and the executable is still named `cholesky`.\n\n**What changed in the decomposition**\n\n- The unblocked sequential algorithm was replaced by a right-looking blocked Cholesky with 64-wide panels. Per panel: the diagonal block is factored by one thread, the panel below it is solved in parallel over rows, and the trailing submatrix update is spread over row-by-column-tile work items with dynamic scheduling, longest rows first.\n- The trailing update uses a transposed copy of the panel so the inner loop is a contiguous, vectorized multiply-add along a row, unrolled over four panel columns.\n- Matrix generation and validation are parallelized over rows with their summation order unchanged, so the input matrix is bit-identical to the original. The validation loop also skips the structurally zero part of L.\n- Worker threads are pinned to CPUs at startup unless the user already set OMP_PROC_BIND or OMP_PLACES. Without this, unpinned 256-thread runs were up to 10x slower and highly variable. The decomposition also caps threads at n/8 so tiny matrices do not pay for hundreds of idle threads.\n- Non-positive-definite input still prints the same error message with the same diagonal index and returns failure. A stress test of 80 runs plus a mid-matrix failure case confirmed this after I fixed a barrier-mismatch hang caused by reading the error flag in the loop condition before the first barrier.\n\n**Results on this 2-socket, 128-core EPYC machine** (validation passed at every size tested, including 1, 7, 63, 64, 65, 129, 1000, 4096)\n\n| n | original | new, 1 thread | new, default threads |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 2048 | 2123 ms | ~480 ms | ~18 ms |\n| 4096 | 17877 ms | | ~100 ms |\n| 8192 | | | ~690 ms, about 265 GFLOPS |\n\nThe result hash printed by `-r` differs from the original because the blocked algorithm sums in a different order. Values agree to roughly 1e-10 relative, well inside the 1e-6 validation tolerance, and the printed sum matches the original to all 18 digits."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":32,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3337,"cache_read_input_tokens":49435,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":3337},"output_tokens":8,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"global","speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"72689534-f2fe-4cd2-a229-b8ddfe927fb2","uuid":"edb44b70-976c-4658-9a46-81e208720f22","timestamp":"2026-10-16T17:43:54.184Z","request_id":"req_011Cg6L8Fzg4yoYGMmmQSCRb"}

No new files were added and the executable is still named `cholesky`.\n\n**What changed in the decomposition**\n\n- The unblocked sequential algorithm was replaced by a right-looking blocked Cholesky with 64-wide panels. Without this, unpinned 256-thread runs were up to 10x slower and highly variable. The decomposition also caps threads at n/8 so tiny matrices do not pay for hundreds of idle threads.\n- Non-positive-definite input still prints the same error message with the same diagonal index and returns failure.
